Org. Setting and Reporting
This position is located in the Department of Economic and Social Affairs (DESA), the Division for Sustainable Development Goals, in the Small Island Developing States (SIDS) Unit. The Division provides leadership in promoting and coordinating implementation of the sustainable development agenda of the United Nations and serves as the global secretariat for the 17 Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) of the 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development and provides substantive inputs to the High-level Political Forum on Sustainable Development. For more information, please visit our website at http://sdgs.un.org.

The Associate Sustainable Development Officer reports to the Chief of the SIDS Unit.


Responsibilities
Within delegated authority, the Associate Sustainable Development Officer will be responsible for the following duties:

Undertakes research, collects, evaluates, and analyses information and data on sustainable development relevant to Small Island Developing States.
Prepares first drafts of assigned analytical studies on sustainable development issues and policies relevant to Small Island Developing States.
Assists in the organization and servicing of expert group meetings, seminars and workshops on sustainable development issues.
Prepares inputs on Small Island Developing States for reports to the High-level Political Forum on Sustainable Development and other intergovernmental bodies.
Follows intergovernmental meetings on sustainable development and SIDS related issues and prepares summary reports and similar material.
Prepares inputs into statements, talking points, including power point presentations, by senior staff to the intergovernmental, inter-agency or expert group meetings on sustainable development and SIDS related issues.
Assists in the organization of panels, round tables on sustainable development issues.
Compiles and analyses data and other information required for the preparation of capacity development projects in the area of sustainable development relevant to SIDS.
Monitors, backstops and assesses the implementation of capacity building projects on SIDS.
Performs other duties as required, including administrative tasks related to the assigned substantive activities.
Undertakes on the job and other training activities, both internally and externally.
Competencies
•    Professionalism: Knowledge and understanding of sustainable development theories, concepts and approaches. Ability to identify and develop sources for data collection. Ability to undertake research, analyse data, make recommendations and write draft reports on sustainable development issues. Knowledge of the UN system in implementing Agenda 21, the Johannesburg Plan of Implementation (JPOI); the Barbados Programme of Action for the Sustainable Development of Small Island Developing States (BPOA), the Mauritius Strategy for the Further Implementation of the Programme of Action for the Sustainable Development of Small Island Developing States (MSI), the Rio+20 outcomes, the SAMOA Pathway and the 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development. Shows pride in work and in achievements; demonstrates professional competence and mastery of subject matter; is conscientious and efficient in meeting commitments, observing deadlines and achieving results; is motivated by professional rather than personal concerns; shows persistence when faced with difficult problems or challenges; remains calm in stressful situations. Takes responsibility for incorporating gender perspectives and ensuring the equal participation of women and men in all areas of work.
•    Planning and Organizing: Develops clear goals that are consistent with agreed strategies; identifies priority activities and assignments; adjusts priorities as required; allocates appropriate amount of time and resources for completing work; foresees risks and allows for contingencies when planning; monitors and adjusts plans and actions as necessary; uses time efficiently.
•    Teamwork: Works collaboratively with colleagues to achieve organizational goals; solicits input by genuinely valuing others' ideas and expertise; is willing to learn from others; places team agenda before personal agenda; supports and acts in accordance with final group decision, even when such decisions may not entirely reflect own position; shares credit for team accomplishments and accepts joint responsibility for team shortcomings.

Eligibility & Suitability Req
Education: A first-level university degree is required; preferably in sustainable development, economics, public policy, public administration, finance, natural resources management, engineering, law, social sciences or related field is desirable.
Experience: Experience in project or programme management and policy-oriented work in a broad range of sustainable development issues is desirable.
Language: English and French are the two working languages of the United Nations. For this post, fluency in English is required. Knowledge of another UN official language is an advantage.

Career Development
YPP Conditions
Languages
English and French are the working languages of the United Nations Secretariat. For this post, English is required. The table below shows the minimum required level for each skill in these languages, according to the UN Language Framework (please consult https://languages.un.org for details).
Required Languages
Language	Reading	Writing	Listening	Speaking
English	UN Level II	UN Level II	UN Level II	UN Level II
Education
A first-level university degree is required; preferably in sustainable development, economics, public policy, public administration, finance, natural resources management, engineering, law, social sciences or related field is desirable.

Org. Setting and Reporting
The position is located in the Office of the Director of the Financing for Sustainable 
Development Office (FSDO) in the Department of Economic and Social Affairs (DESA). 
The FSDO works to advance policy and action on financing for sustainable development to 
support Member States and other stakeholders in achieving the 2030 Agenda for 
Sustainable Development, the Sevilla Commitment and the Addis Ababa Action Agenda 
on Financing for Development by providing thought leadership and agenda sharing, 
promoting multistakeholder dialogue, identifying policy options and best practices, and 
offering strategic advice and support for capacity development in key areas. More 
information can be found at https://financing.desa.un.org/.


The Associate Economics Affairs Officer reports to the Director of FSDO and will focus 
primarily on international development cooperation. 

Responsibilities
Within delegated authority, the duties of an Associate Economic Affairs 
Officer are:


Economic and/or sector analysis: 
• Collects, evaluates, interprets, analyses specified statistics and qualitative socio economic information obtained from a variety of sources and pertaining to particular 
sectors, countries, regions or development topics, including through analytical work on 
international development cooperation.
• Maintains databases and programmes for database management and organizes non quantitative information.
• Prepares briefing notes, inputs to speeches and similar material on economic or sector 
issues in a form that is meaningful to non-specialists; 
• Prepares first drafts of sector, country or regional components of analytical studies on 
development issues and policies, including analytical inputs related to trends and 
progress in international development cooperation.
• Assists in the organization and servicing of expert group meetings, advisory group 
processes, panel discussions, seminars and similar consultations on development 
issues. 

Intergovernmental support:
• Prepares inputs for reports to intergovernmental bodies.
• Follows intergovernmental meetings and prepares summary reports and similar 
material, including the ECOSOC Forum on Financing for Development and related 
follow-up processes.
• Prepares inputs to statements by members of the Bureau and Secretariat staff to such 
meetings.

• Assists in the organization of panels, round tables etc on development issues for inter governmental processes. 
 

Technical cooperation: 
• Compiles and analyses the data and other information necessary for the preparation of 
technical cooperation project proposals in the area of economic development.
• Prepares documentation for technical cooperation programmes and projects.
• Contributes to the preparation of capacity development activities related to 
effectiveness of development cooperation.
• Monitors, backstops and assesses the implementation of technical cooperation 
programmes and projects, including through the use of data and country-level evidence. 
 

General: 
• Performs other related duties as required, including a variety of administrative tasks 
necessary for the final delivery of the work unit’s services; 
• Undertakes on-the-job and other training activities, both internally and externally. 
• Contributes to the collection and analysis of data as well as identification of trends or 
patterns and provides draft insights through graphs, charts, tables and reports using data 
visualization methods for data-driven planning, decision-making, presentation and 
reporting.

Competencies
• Professionalism: Knowledge of economic theories and their application in the area of 
international development cooperation, including issues related to system-wide coherence, 
effectiveness and financing for sustainable development. Ability to identify and develop
sources for data collection, including through surveys and consultations with diverse 
stakeholders. Ability to undertake research, analyse data, make recommendations and write 
reports. Shows pride in work and in achievements; demonstrates professional competence 
and mastery of subject matter; is conscientious and efficient in meeting commitments, 
observing deadlines and achieving results; is motivated by professional rather than personal 
concerns; shows persistence when faced with difficult problems or challenges; remains calm 
in stressful situations. Takes responsibility for incorporating gender perspectives and 
ensuring the equal participation of women and men in all areas of work. Able to analyze and 
interpret data in support of decision-making and convey resulting information to 
management.


• Teamwork: Works collaboratively with colleagues to achieve organizational goals; solicits 
input by genuinely valuing others’ ideas and expertise; is willing to learn from others; places 
team agenda before personal agenda; supports and acts in accordance with final group 
decision, even when such decisions may not entirely reflect own position; shares credit for 
team accomplishments and accepts joint responsibility for team shortcomings.
 

• Planning& Organizing: Develops clear goals that are consistent with agreed strategies; 
identifies priority activities and assignments; adjusts priorities as required; allocates 
appropriate amount of time and resources for completing work; foresees risks and allows for 
contingencies when planning; monitors and adjusts plans and actions as necessary; uses time 
efficiently. 

Eligibility & Suitability Req
Education: A first-level university degree is required; preferably in Economics or related field is desirable.

Experience: Experience in economic or policy making is desirable.


Language: English and French are the two working languages of the United Nations. For this post, fluency in English is required. Knowledge of another UN official language is an advantage.

Career Development
YPP Conditions
Languages
English and French are the working languages of the United Nations Secretariat. For this post, English is required. The table below shows the minimum required level for each skill in these languages, according to the UN Language Framework (please consult https://languages.un.org for details).
Required Languages
Language	Reading	Writing	Listening	Speaking
English	UN Level II	UN Level II	UN Level II	UN Level II

Org. Setting and Reporting
This position is located in the Demographic and Social Statistics Branch of the Statistics Division, Department of Economic and Social Affairs (DESA). The Associate Statistician reports to the Chief of the Social and Gender Statistics Section.

DESA is a vital interface between global policies in the economic, social and environmental spheres and national action. Its work is guided by the universal, integrated and transformative 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development, along with a set of 17 Sustainable Development Goals and 169 associated targets adopted by the UN General Assembly on 25 September 2015. 

 

Responsibilities
Within delegated authority, the Associate Statistician will be responsible for the following duties:

Participates in the collection, evaluation, analysis, compilation and dissemination of  social and gender statistics and data from Member States by selecting methods of data collection, selecting and implementing appropriate methods for validating collected data and processing data for incorporation into statistical database(s); 
Assist in capacity development and technical support to countries on social and gender statistics production, and review relevant country practices;
Assist in the development or revision of standards for data and metadata on gender, international migration, time-use and other social statistics, including statistical concepts, definitions and classifications;
Conduct methodological research, analyze, and present information gathered from various data sources on assigned topics; 
Prepare draft technical documents for international, intergovernmental and expert group meetings, and assist in drafting relevant technical reports;
Assist in the organization and conduction of seminars, workshops and expert meetings;
Collaborate with general service and professional staff in the section, branch and division;
Maintain close liaison and cooperation with relevant counterparts in national and international institutions, including headquarters, regional commissions, specialized agencies and national governments;
Perform other duties as assigned.
 

 

Competencies
Professionalism (without gender indicator) :

Professionalism: 
- Demonstrate skills necessary to identify, extract, analyse and disseminate statistical data.

Planning and organizing: 
-Develops clear goals that are consistent with agreed strategies. 
-Ability to establish priority, and to plan, coordinate and monitor own workplan. 
-Allocates appropriate amount of time and resources for completing work. 
-Foresees risks and allows for contingencies when planning. 

Technological awareness: 
-Solid IT skills and their application to statistical work, including proficiency in MS Office, data science and relevant software.
- Keeps abreast of available technology
- Understands applicability and limitations of technology to the work of the Office
- Actively seeks to apply technology to appropriate tasks
-Shows willingness to learn new technology.
 

Eligibility & Suitability Req
Qualifications:

Education: A first-level university degree is required, preferably in [field(s) of statistics, mathematics, economics, sociology, data science or related field is desirable. Candidates for this position must have passed the YPP or G to P Examination.

Experience:  Experience in the field of statistics, population affairs or social affairs is desirable.

Language: English and French are the two working languages of the United Nations. For this post, fluency in English is required.  Knowledge of another UN official language is an advantage

Career Development
YPP Conditions
Languages
English and French are the working languages of the United Nations Secretariat. For this post, English is required. The table below shows the minimum required level for each skill in these languages, according to the UN Language Framework (please consult https://languages.un.org for details).

Org. Setting and Reporting
This position is located in the French Language Unit (FLU), in the News and Media Division (NMD) of the Department of Global Communications (DGC).  The News Section produces daily news and feature stories, programmes, special series and creates audio content using live and recorded audio from meetings and events, and multimedia pieces for the UN News digital platform, incorporating audio and video elements, as well as social media content. Through these products UN News provide daily and weekly content in eight (8) languages (Arabic, Chinese, English, French, Spanish, and Russian). Incumbent reports to the Chief of FLU.

Responsibilities
Within delegated authority the Associate Public Information Officer is responsible for the following duties:  

Contributes to timely and accurate coverage of key United Nations developments at Headquarters and around the world, including through regular weekend duty shifts and evenings as needed.
Drafts/compiles a specific type or types (e.g. print, broadcast, etc.), of information communications products for target audiences, to include digital content, press releases, media packets and reports, brochures, briefings, video clips, newsletters, websites, etc.
Researches and drafts news and feature stories under the direction of senior editorial staff and provides assistance with interviews, developing and generating news and multimedia feature materials, as well as producing audio and video content to multiple daily deadlines.
Works with photo and digital assets to illustrate multimedia content. Assists with social media for the UN News sites.
In consultation with others identifies and proposes information opportunities, activities and approaches, taking into account the situation/topic and target audience.
Monitors a wide range of UN developments and news sources, including official press releases, statements, news conferences, briefings and meetings of various bodies; conducting interviews, transcribing recordings and creating short multimedia pieces, for use as related content.
Draws on a wide variety of monitoring tools and keeps a close watch on analytics for the news website.
Helps to maintain and update content of the UN News website, including focus pages, interviews, photo stories and a wide variety of related digital content, as well as for related social media sites for the relevant UN News platform as needed.
Responds to a variety of inquires and information requests internally and externally; prepares related correspondence.
Performs other duties as assigned.
Competencies
Professionalism: Skills in digital journalism for audio, video and online news production purposes, political awareness, judgment and solid knowledge of international current affairs. Possesses research and analytical skills. Ability to make effective on-air presentations. Shows pride in work and in achievements. Demonstrates professional competence and mastery of subject matter. Is conscientious and efficient in observing deadlines and achieving results. Is motivated by professional rather than personal concerns. Shows persistence when faced with difficult problems or challenges. Remains calm in stressful situations. Takes responsibility for incorporating gender perspectives and ensuring the equal participation of women and men in all areas of work.

Communication:  Speaks and writes clearly and effectively; listens to others, correctly interprets messages from others and responds appropriately; asks questions to clarify, and exhibits interest in having two-way communication; tailors language, tone, style and format to match audience; demonstrates openness in sharing information and keeping people informed.

Teamwork:  Works collaboratively with colleagues to achieve organizational goals; solicits input by genuinely valuing others’ ideas and expertise; is willing to learn from others; places team agenda before personal agenda; supports and acts in accordance with final group decision, even when such decisions may not entirely reflect own position; shares credit for team accomplishments and accepts joint responsibility for team shortcomings.

Eligibility & Suitability Req
Education: A first-level university degree is required; preferably in communication, journalism, international relations, public relations or related area.

Experience: A minimum of two years of progressively responsible experience in public information, journalism, international relations, public administration or related area is desirable. Experience in writing news to daily deadlines and multimedia broadcast journalism in the relevant language of the news team, including knowledge of digital content publishing is desirable. Experience managing social media platforms and analytics is desirable. Experience with on-air presentations is desirable.

Language: English and French are the two working languages of the United Nations. For this post, fluency in English and French is required. Knowledge of another UN official language is an advantage.

Career Development
YPP Conditions
Languages
English and French are the working languages of the United Nations Secretariat. For this post, English and French are required. Org. Setting and Reporting
This post is located in the Asia and the Pacific Division in the Departments of Political and Peacebuilding Affairs and Peace Operations (DPPA-DPO). Under the general supervision of the Director of Asia and Pacific Division, the incumbent reports to a Senior Political Affairs Officer/Team Leader.

Responsibilities
Under the supervision of the Team Leader for South and Southeast Asia, the Associate Political Affairs Officer will be responsible for the following duties:


• Analyses information contained in communications and publications from different sources, including the press; maintains up-to-date knowledge of
developments relating to political issues in a number of countries as assigned, or specified aspects of a subject matter;
• Assists senior officials with briefing notes, background papers and talking points.
• Prepares summaries from a wide variety of sources and drafts a variety of correspondence, and sections of reports.
• Monitors political, socio-economic, and other politically relevant developments in an assigned area.
• Collaborates closely with other team members and provides input into political, humanitarian and economic efforts and programmes.
• Creates databases that track project progress and provides other information relevant to assigned area.
• Helps formulate project proposals and ensures their implementation.
• Performs other related duties as required.

Competencies
Professionalism: Ability to identify and analyze political, ethnic, racial, social and economic problems that cause civil unrest in a country or geographic area. Ability to develop sources for data collection. Shows pride in work and in achievements; demonstrates professional competence and mastery of subject matter; is conscientious and efficient in meeting commitments, observing deadlines and achieving results; is motivated by professional rather than personal concerns; shows persistence when faced with difficult problems or challenges; remains calm in stressful situations. Takes responsibility for incorporating gender perspectives and ensuring the equal participation of women and men in all areas of work.
 

Communication: Speaks and writes clearly and effectively. Listens to others, correctly interprets messages from others and responds appropriately. Asks questions to clarify, and exhibits interest in having two-way communication. Tailors language, tone, style and format to match the audience. Demonstrates openness in sharing information and keeping people informed.
 

Planning and organizing: Develops clear goals that are consistent with agreed strategies. Identifies priority activities and assignments; adjusts priorities as required. Allocates appropriate amount of time and resources for completing work. Foresees risks and allows for contingencies when planning. Monitors and adjusts plans and actions as necessary. Uses time efficiently.

Eligibility & Suitability Req
Education:
A first-level university degree in political science, international relations, international economics, law, public administration or other related area is required.
 

Experience:
 

MRP is mandatory for YPP staff with two years of service in their initial assignment. At least one year of YPP service is required.

Experience working on peace and security, or related issues is desirable.
Experience in monitoring political, peace and security and related developments in a geographic area of responsibility is desirable.
Experience in writing documentation for senior officials, correspondence, country specific/regional analysis is desirable.
Experience in organizing large multi-stakeholder meetings and/or events is desirable.
Experience in backstopping field presences is desirable.
Experience in programmatic and administrative work is desirable.

this post, fluency in English is required. Knowledge of another UN official language is an
advantage.

Job Code Title
ASSOCIATE FINANCE AND BUDGET OFFICER
Level

Org. Setting and Reporting
This position is located in the Financial Policy and Internal Controls Service (FPICS) of the Finance Division (FD), Office of Programme Planning, Finance and Budget (OPPFB) in the Department of Management Strategy, Policy and Compliance (DMSPC).

The Finance Division is responsible for securing the financing of the Organization's expenses, pursuant to Article 17 of the Charter of the United Nations, and to ensure sound, effective and efficient integrated financial management of and reporting on United Nations resources, supported by a strong internal control environment.

FPICS leads the Secretariat-wide implementation of the internal control framework and adapts the internal controls and financial policies needed to ensure prudent management of resources within a framework of decentralized delegation of authority. The Associate Finance and Budget Officer reports to the Section Chief.

Responsibilities
Within delegated authority, the Associate Finance & Budget Officer will be responsible for the following duties:

Medium-term plan:

-Reviews, analyses and finalizes the preparation of the medium-term plan and its revisions on the basis of departmental submissions, taking into account recommendations and decisions of specialized intergovernmental bodies.

-Provides advice and support to relevant intergovernmental and expert bodies in their review of the medium-term plan and its revisions; assists in securing approval from the General Assembly.

-Ensures legislative mandates are accurately translated into programme budgets.

Budget preparation:

-Reviews, analyses and assists in the finalization of cost estimates and budget proposals, in terms of staff and non-staff requirements from (a) Departments/offices at Headquarters and in the field, including programmatic aspects; (b) smaller-sized peacekeeping operations; ensure compliance with the medium-term plan and other legislative mandates.

-Reviews and analyses programme budget/financial implications to the Security Council statements in light of additional mandates.

-Assists managers in the elaboration of resource requirements for budget submissions.

-Provides support to relevant intergovernmental and expert bodies in their budget review; assists in securing approval from the General Assembly.

Budget administration:

-Monitors budget implementation and recommends reallocation of funds as necessary.

-Monitors expenditures to ensure that they remain within authorized levels.

-Reviews requisitions for goods and services to ensure (a) correct objects of expenditure have been charged, and (b)availability of funds.

-Administers and monitors extra-budgetary resources, including review of agreements and cost plans, ensuring compliance with regulations and rules and established policies and procedures.

-Assists in the preparation of budget performance submissions.

-Reviews and assists in finalization of budget performance reports, analyzing variances between approved budgets and actual expenditures.

Finance:

-Analyses and reviews/evaluates enterprise risk management registers from departments/offices and Peacekeeping Missions. Identifies inconsistencies with the Secretariat's internal control framework resolving them in a timely and effective manner.

-Approves/Confirms that proposed obligations or expenditures for services, facilities supplies and equipment expenses, as well as those pertaining to personnel, are in accordance with the Financial Regulations and Rules of the UN, relevant appropriations and staffing tables and corresponding approved allotments/appropriations.

-Assists, prepares, or consolidates the reports on cases of fraud and presumptive fraud for inclusion in the UN Financial Statements to be audited. Responds to internal/external audit observations.

-Assists in ensuring that accurate and complete reporting and internal control systems are functioning and that all relevant records are maintained.

-Plans and works with systems units with respect to relevant risk compliance reports. 

-Researches and analyses information needed to respond to audit findings.

General:

-Keeps up-to-date on documents/reports/guidelines that have a bearing on matters related to programme and/or peacekeeping budgets, ensuring compliance with intergovernmental recommendations and decisions as well as with United Nations policies and procedures;

-Keeps up-to-date on current financial regulations/professional publications and accepted principles;

-Collects, organises, and analyses significant amounts of information with attention to detail and interprets trends and patterns to support decision making;

-Performs other related duties, as assigned.

Competencies
Professionalism: Knowledge of financial budgetary, risk management and internal control principles and practices and financial administration of resources. Ability to develop budgetary and financial policies and procedures. Ability to conduct independent research and analysis, identify issues, formulate options, and make conclusions and recommendations. Is able to prepare reports, formulating positions on issues, articulating options concisely conveying maximum necessary information making and defending recommendations. Familiarity with the UN Financial regulations and Rules. Shows pride in work and in achievements; demonstrates professional competence and mastery of subject matter; is conscientious and efficient in meeting commitments, observing deadlines, and achieving results; is motivated by professional rather than personal concerns; shows persistence when faced with difficult problems or challenges; remains calm in stressful situations. Takes responsibility for incorporating gender perspectives and ensuring the equal participation of women and men in all areas of work.

Communication: Speaks and writes clearly and effectively. Listens to others, correctly interprets messages from others and responds appropriately. Asks questions to clarify, and exhibits interest in having two-way communication. Tailors language, tone, style and format to match the audience. Demonstrates openness in sharing information and keeping people informed.

Planning and organizing: Develops clear goals that are consistent with agreed strategies. Identifies priority activities and assignments; adjusts priorities as required. Allocates appropriate amount of time and resources for completing work. Foresees risks and allows for contingencies when planning. Monitors and adjusts plans and actions as necessary. Uses time efficiently.

Eligibility & Suitability Req
Education:

A first-level university degree is required, preferably in business administration, finance, accounting, or related field.

Experience:

Progressively responsible experience in accounting, finance, administration, budget, business administration or related area is desirable.

Experience in preparing written outputs including background papers, analytical notes, reports etc. is desirable.

Experience in risk management and on implementing an internal control framework such as the 2013 COSO internal control integrated framework or equivalent is desirable.

Language: English and French are the two working languages of the United Nations. For this post, fluency in English is required. Knowledge of another UN official language is an advantage.

Career Development
YPP Conditions
Languages
English and French are the working languages of the United Nations Secretariat. For this post, English is required. The table below shows the minimum required level for each skill in these languages, according to the UN Language Framework (please consult https://languages.un.org for details).

English	UN Level II	UN Level II	UN Level II	UN Level II

Org. Setting and Reporting
This position is located in the Western Africa Division of the Departments of Political and Peacebuilding Affairs and Peace Operations (DPPA-DPO) at Headquarters. The Associate Political Affairs Officer reports to the Senior Political Affairs Officer/Team Leader of West Africa Team, under the overall guidance of the Director and Deputy Director for the Western Africa Division.

Responsibilities
Within delegated authority, the Associate Political Affairs Officer will be responsible for the following duties:

• Monitors political, peace and security events in assigned countries in the Western Africa region; and assesses national and regional trends which might affect the political situation in the countries within his/her purview in the Western Africa Division.
• Prepares, in coordination with the Team Leader and under the overall guidance of the Director and Deputy Director for the Western Africa Division, briefings, talking points, reports, weekly updates and inputs to the Office of the Assistant Secretary-General for Africa, the Office of the Under Secretary-General for Political and Peacebuilding Affairs and the Executive Office of the Secretary-General.
• Drafts correspondence, notes, reports, memoranda, and responses to queries for the review of the Senior Political Affairs Officer.
• Develops and maintains professional relationships, which support and facilitate the work of the Western Africa Division, including with representatives of national governments and international organizations, and other concerned partners, including consultants, business executives, and civil society and academics.
• Arranges and participates in technical, fact-finding, and assessment missions.
• Attends meetings and consultations of the Security Council, the General Assembly, subsidiary organizations, negotiating bodies, conferences, meetings, and/or special missions on issues relevant to the Western Africa region.
• Organizes, supports, or participates in inter-agency meetings and crisis mechanisms relevant to the countries assigned in the Western Africa region.
• Performs other related duties as required.

Competencies

               Finance:

•             Reviews, approves, reconciles and processes a range of accounting transactions on a regular and recurring basis.

•             Plans and works with systems units, in the installation and administration of accounting systems. 

•             Researches and analyses information needed to respond to audit findings. 

               

               Budget administration:

ECA's mission is to deliver ideas and actions for an empowered and transformed Africa; informed by the 2030 Agenda and Agenda 2063. The mission will be guided by ECA's five new strategic directions which are advancing ECA's position as a premier knowledge institution that builds on its unique position and privilege to bring global solutions to the continent's problems and take local solutions to the continent; developing macroeconomic and structural policy options to accelerate economic diversification and job creation; designing and implementing innovative financing models for infrastructure, and for human, physical and social assets for a transforming Africa; contributing solutions to regional and transboundary challenges, with a focus on peace security and social inclusion as an important development nexus; advocating Africa's position at the global level and developing regional responses as a contribution to global governance issues.


Responsibilities

1. Budget Preparation and Budget Administration

h. Assists with the preparation of cash-flow statements for multiple accounts on a monthly basis. Reviews and evaluates financial reports for ECA with a view to identifying anomalies and unusual trends. Initiates corrective action.

k. Assists in ensuring that accurate and complete accounting, reporting and internal control systems are functioning and that all relevant records are maintained.
l. Assists in ensuring the compliance of the Financial Rules and Regulations and the appropriate application of IPSAS.

The Associate Economic Affairs Officer is responsible for conducting economic analysis and research, gathering data, promoting the organization’s positions, and supporting relationships with external stakeholders. The role engages with a wide range of partners throughout the research and policy process—collecting data, consulting on methodologies, disseminating findings, representing the organization, and strengthening collaborative relationships. This position is located in the Regional Integration and Economic Diversification Section (RIEDS) of the Regional Integration and Trade Division (RITD) of the United Nations Economic Commission for Africa (ECA) in Addis Ababa, Ethiopia. The Associate Economics Affairs Officer works under the direct supervision of the Chief of Section and under the overall guidance of the Director of the Regional Integration and Trade Division.

Responsibilities
Support the collection, integration, and analysis of data, contribute to identifying trends or patterns, and prepare draft insights through graphs, charts, tables, and reports using data visualization methods to support data‑driven planning, decision‑making, presentation, and reporting.
Analyze data on regional, sub-regional and national economic indicators, including macroeconomic indicators, using statistical tools in collaboration with internal and external research partners, to generate insights that inform policies and programme design.
Maintain and regularly update socio-economic databases by validating, organizing, and integrating new data to support timely analysis and inform the work of multiple organizational units.
Contribute to econometric and statistical analysis by supporting the interpretation of outputs and providing inputs to scenario planning, evidence-based decision-making, and programme design.
Contribute to the design and estimation of econometric models by preparing datasets, supporting model specification, and conducting analysis to investigate the effects of economic policies on developmental outcomes.
Contribute to policy advisory missions by conducting initial analysis, preparing background materials, and supporting inclusive economic and development planning processes.
Contribute to facilitating dialogue and coordination with national, regional, and external stakeholders to support inclusive policymaking and capacity-building in regional economics by supporting engagement processes and providing analytical inputs.
Contribute to studies on regional integration, trade corridors, regional value chains, regional investment clusters, and cross-border linkages by analysis and synthesizing findings to support regional development initiatives and cooperation frameworks.
Contribute to the analysis of industrial and innovation clusters by integrating data, literature, and stakeholder inputs to support regional growth through industry cooperation and new technologies, while also ensuring environmental protection.
Contribute to the development and implementation of models and frameworks that support economic planning and transformation in the context of climate resilience, nature-positive outcomes, pollution reduction, and sustainable natural resource management through conducting data analysis and generating analytical findings.
Provide analytical inputs and supporting content for the preparation of technical reports, policy briefs and presentations, by synthesizing data from diverse sources and highlighting relevant economic trends.
Contribute to the implementation, monitoring, and evaluation of programmes and projects on economic affairs by supporting coordination, tracking progress, and providing inputs to facilitate timely delivery.
Contribute to the planning and monitoring of regional, sub-regional and country development programmes by supporting design processes and assisting in progress reviews to ensure alignment with national priorities and international commitments.
Contribute to the design and implementation of technical cooperation activities in partnership with country stakeholders, supporting alignment with local needs and development priorities.
Contribute to cross-departmental preparations for major UN processes by supporting coordination efforts, information gathering, and assisting in aligning outputs with intergovernmental agendas.
Conduct research and collect data for analytical support to relevant intergovernmental bodies and assist by synthesizing technical insights into accessible documents for diverse global audiences.
Contribute to the preparation of briefing notes, talking points, policy statements, speeches, and presentations by synthesizing inputs and supporting analytical content for high-level engagements and intergovernmental processes.
Contribute to the organization of technical meetings and stakeholder workshops by supporting agenda development, stakeholder coordination, and documentation of discussions to facilitate consensus-building and capture lessons learned.
Contribute to preparation of notes, background papers, talking points, speeches, and other correspondence for high-level meetings involving UN agencies, Member States, and stakeholders, for communication, outreach and advocacy purposes.

The Economic and Social Commission for Western Asia (ESCWA) forms part of the United Nations Secretariat and, like the other regional commissions, operates under the supervision of the United Nations Economic and Social Council. ESCWA comprises 21 Arab countries in Western Asia and North Africa. ESCWA provides a framework for the formulation and harmonisation of sectoral policies for member countries, a platform for congress and coordination, a home for expertise and knowledge, and an information observatory. ESCWA activities are coordinated with the divisions and main offices of the Headquarters of the United Nations, specialised agencies, and international and regional organizations, including the League of Arab States and its subsidiary bodies, and the Gulf Cooperation Council. 

This position is in Cluster 4 (Statistics, the information society and technology) which acts as a pioneer and multi-stakeholder regional platform for deliberation, consensus-building, peer learning, and policy solutions to strengthen the institutional framework for official statistics in the Arab region and enhance the production and use of harmonized, timely and reliable statistics for evidence-based policymaking; to enable the measurement of national and regional progress towards the implementation of the 2030 Agenda, and advance information society in the Arab region and promote the integration of technology and innovation for inclusive and sustainable development.


The Associate Programme Officer will report directly to the Cluster Lead.

Responsibilities
Within delegated authority, the Associate Programme Officer will be responsible for the following duties: 

•    Works with key clients to facilitate the development, implementation and evaluation of assigned programmes/projects, etc.; monitors and analyzes specific aspects of programme/project development and implementation; reviews relevant documents and reports; identifies problems and issues to be addressed and recommends corrective actions; liaises with relevant parties; identifies and tracks follow-up actions.

•    Carries out basic research on selected aspects of programmes, operations and other activities, etc., to include collecting, analyzing and presenting statistical data and other information gathered from diverse sources and contributes to activities supporting the modernization of official statistics, including the use and integration of traditional and non-traditional data sources. 

•    Provides substantive support for policy coordination and evaluation functions, including the review and analysis of emerging issues and trends, participation in evaluations or research activities and studies. 

•    Assists in performing consulting assignments, in collaboration with the client, by planning facilitating workshops, through other interactive sessions and assisting in developing the action plan the client will use to manage the change.

•    Participates in survey and other initiatives; assists with design of data collection tools and the identification and use of relevant traditional and non-traditional data sources; reviews, analyzes and interprets responses, identifies problems/issues and prepares preliminary conclusions.   

•    Contributes to the preparation of various written outputs, e.g. draft background papers, analytical notes, sections of reports and studies, inputs to publications, etc.

•    Provides administrative and substantive support to consultative and other meetings, conferences, etc., to include proposing agenda topics, identifying and proposed participants, preparation of background documents and presentations, handling logistics, etc.

•    Undertakes outreach activities; participates in the development of training workshops, seminars, etc.; participates in and makes presentations on assigned topics/activities.

•    Participates in field missions, including provision of substantive and administrative support, data collection, etc.

•    Coordinates activities related to budget funding (programme/project preparation and submissions, progress reports, financial statements, etc.) and preparation of related documents/reports (pledging, work programme, programme budget, etc.).

•    Contribute to the collection, integration and analysis of data from diverse sources, identify trends or patterns and provide draft insights through graphs, charts, tables and reports using data visualization methods for data-driven planning, decision-making, presentation and reporting.

•    Performs other duties as required.

Competencies
•    Professionalism:  Knowledge and understanding of theories, concepts and approaches relevant to official statistics, data and statistical modernization. Familiarity with statistical production processes and with the use of traditional and non-traditional data sources for the production and analysis of statistics. Ability to identify issues, analyse and participate in the resolution of issues/problems. Ability to assist with data collection, integration and analysis using various methods. Conceptual analytical and evaluative skills to conduct independent research and analysis, including  familiarity with and experience in the use of various research sources, including electronic sources on the internet, intranet and other databases.  Ability to apply judgment in the context of assignments given, plan own work and manage conflicting priorities.  Shows pride in work and in achievements; demonstrates professional competence and mastery of subject matter; is conscientious and efficient in meeting commitments, observing deadlines and achieving results; is motivated by professional rather than personal concerns; shows persistence when faced with difficult problems or challenges; remains calm in stressful situations. Takes responsibility for incorporating gender perspectives and ensuring the equal participation of women and men in all areas of work.
 

•    Communication:  Speaks and writes clearly and effectively; listens to others, correctly interprets messages from others and responds appropriately; asks questions to clarify, and exhibits interest in having two-way communication; tailors language, tone, style and format to match audience; demonstrates openness in sharing information and keeping people informed.
 

•    Teamwork:  Works collaboratively with colleagues to achieve organizational goals; solicits input by genuinely valuing others’ ideas and expertise; is willing to learn from others; places team agenda before personal agenda; supports and acts in accordance with final group decision, even when such decisions may not entirely reflect own position; shares credit for team accomplishments and accepts joint responsibility for team shortcomings.

Eligibility & Suitability Req
Education: 

A first-level university degree is required; preferably in business administration, statistics, economics, data science, mathematics, computer science, management or a related field is desirable. 

Experience:  

A minimum of two years of progressively responsible experience in project/programme management, official statistics, data analysis, or related area is desirable. 

Experience in statistical modernization, including work with traditional and/or non-traditional data sources, is desirable.

Experience with statistical software, data management, data visualization or other tools relevant to the production and analysis of official statistics is desirable.

Language: 

English and French are the two working languages of the United Nations. For this post, fluency in English is required.

Org. Setting and Reporting
The Independent Institution on Missing Persons in the Syrian Arab Republic (IIMP) was established by the General Assembly in its resolution 77/301, adopted on 29 June 2023. Please note that while the position is currently located in Geneva, Switzerland, the duty station may be subject to change. Applicants should be aware that the position may be relocated to Damascus, Syria, or to another location relevant to the Institution's operations. By applying, candidates confirm their willingness to accept such a reassignment in accordance with UN regulations and rules, should such a decision be made. The incumbent reports to the Human Rights Officer or his/her designated official in the Search and Identification Section.

Responsibilities
Within delegated authority, the Associate Investigator may be responsible for the following duties: (These duties are generic and may not be performed by all Associate Investigators.)

Assists in the conduct of investigations into serious breaches of international humanitarian law, such as genocide, complicity in genocide, crimes against humanity, conspiracy to commit genocide, war crimes, rape and sexual assaults, as well as investigations concerning missing persons.
Gathers evidence through witness interviews and enquiries with law enforcement agencies, NGOs and humanitarian organizations.
Records statements from survivors, victims, witnesses and relatives of missing persons.
Assists in forensic projects, including exhumations, as and when required.
Takes part in search and seizure missions where required.
Contributes to the collection and analysis of data, as well as the identification of trends or patterns, including those relevant to missing persons cases, and provides draft insights through graphs, charts, tables and reports using data visualization methods for data-driven planning, decision-making, presentation and reporting.
Competencies
Professionalism: -Knowledge of theories, concepts and approaches relevant to criminal investigations. Good research, analytical and problem-solving skills, including ability to identify and participate in the resolution of issues/problems. Knowledge of various research methodologies and sources, including electronic sources on the Internet, intranet and other databases. Ability to apply good judgment in the context of assignments given, ability to gather evidence and interview suspects and witnesses. -Shows pride in work and in achievements. -Demonstrates professional competence and mastery of subject matter. Is conscientious and efficient in meeting commitments, observing deadlines and achieving results. Is motivated by professional rather than personal concerns. -Shows persistence when faced with difficult problems or challenges; remains calm in stressful situations.-Takes responsibility for incorporating gender perspectives and ensuring the equal participation of women and men in all areas of work. 

Communication: -Speaks and writes clearly and effectively. -Listens to others, correctly interprets messages from others and responds appropriately. -Asks questions to clarify, and exhibits interest in having two-way communication. -Tailors language, tone, style and format to match the audience. -Demonstrates openness in sharing information and keeping people informed. 

Teamwork: -Works collaboratively with colleagues to achieve organizational goals. -Solicits input by genuinely valuing other's ideas and expertise; is willing to learn from others. -Places team agenda before personal agenda. Supports and acts in accordance with final group decision, even when such decisions may not entirely reflect own position. -Shares credit for team accomplishments and accepts joint responsibility for team shortcomings.

Eligibility & Suitability Req
Education: A first-level university degree is required; preferably in international law, criminal investigation or related field. 

Experience: Experience in investigative work related to human rights violations, international crimes, or other serious criminal offences is desirable. Experience working in direct contact with victims, survivors, and/or families of victims is desirable. 

Language: English and French are the two working languages of the United Nations. For this post, fluency in English and Arabic is required.

Contribute to guidance and professional support to civil society actors on integrating human rights into protection, rule-of-law, development, transitional justice and accountability initiatives.
Provide research, coordination and follow-up support to treaty bodies, special procedures, the Universal Periodic Review and commissions of inquiry.

Org. Setting and Reporting
This position is located in the Office of the United Nations High Commissioner for Human Rights, Regional Office for the Middle East and North Africa (ROMENA), in Beirut, Lebanon. The Human Rights Officer will work under the overall supervision of the Regional Representative and the direct supervision and guidance of a Human Rights Officer at ROMENA. 

Responsibilities
Within delegated authority, the Associate Human Rights Officer will be responsible for the following duties: 

 

Monitor the implementation of international human rights law and international humanitarian law, and support follow-up on recommendations from human rights mechanisms, including treaty bodies, the Universal Periodic Review, special procedures and investigative mechanisms, to advance compliance and accountability.
Collect, document and analyse evidence-based information on civil, cultural, economic, social and political rights, including the right to development, within assigned thematic or geographic areas to support in-depth analysis, early warning, accountability, and informed decision-making, ensuring that human rights data is discoverable, interoperable and appropriately shared, including from gender and leave-no-one-behind perspectives.
Conduct human rights monitoring and field verification missions, including assessments and early-warning exercises, in line with UN standards and methodologies, and draft substantive analytical reports containing findings, trends and actionable recommendations.
Support fact-finding and investigative missions documenting violations of international human rights and humanitarian law, ensuring adherence to victim- and witness-protection standards and facilitating collaboration across components.
Draft, review and contribute to human rights reports, briefings, legal and policy analyses and other communications, and prepare analytical papers that inform strategies, policies, programmes and decision-making, with integration of gender and leave-no-one-behind considerations.
Collect, verify and analyse multi-source information on thematic and country-specific human rights situations, ensuring data quality, methodological compliance and gender-responsive analysis.
Prepare analytical products, including lessons learned and trend analyses, to inform targeted human rights strategies, crisis prevention and response, and policy and programme development.
Provide technical research and substantive support to advisory processes with national counterparts, including reviewing draft legislation, policies and security-sector reforms to ensure alignment with international human rights standards.
Provide substantive professional support to national and regional counterparts in strengthening human rights systems, institutions and frameworks, including complaints mechanisms, victim and witness support services, engagement with international human rights mechanisms, and implementation of recommendations.
Support the integration of international human rights norms and standards into political, peace and security, humanitarian, development and economic frameworks and processes, contributing to efforts to address impunity and structural human rights challenges.
Contribute to advocacy and participatory processes related to intergovernmental negotiations, public forums and peace processes, supporting the integration of human rights and anti-discrimination standards.
Provide technical support on administration of justice and security-sector issues, including trial and detention monitoring, transitional justice initiatives and vetting processes, through analysis and technical inputs.
Contribute to guidance and professional support to civil society actors on integrating human rights into protection, rule-of-law, development, transitional justice and accountability initiatives.
Provide research, coordination and follow-up support to treaty bodies, special procedures, the Universal Periodic Review and commissions of inquiry.
Provide professional research, analysis and policy advisory support on human rights issues related to economic policy and sustainable development, including poverty, inequality and development financing, contributing inputs to UN analytical and cooperation frameworks.
Engage with youth networks to support advocacy for youth rights and meaningful participation in civic, political and decision-making processes.
Support effective communication and public information efforts by contributing drafting, technical and coordination inputs to amplify human rights messaging.
Competencies
PROFESSIONALISM: Knowledge of human rights issues and ability to identify related problems in their political, ethnic, racial, gender equality and socio-economic dimensions. Knowledge of institutional mandates of the Human Rights Council and its subsidiary mechanisms, prevailing policies and procedures. Research and analytical skills, including ability to identify and participate in the resolution of human rights issues. Ability to identify sources for data collection, analyze information and draft human rights reports. Shows pride in work and in achievements; demonstrates professional competence and mastery of subject matter; is conscientious and efficient in meeting commitments, observing deadlines and achieving results; is motivated by professional rather than personal concerns; shows persistence when faced with difficult problems or challenges; remains calm in stressful situations. Takes responsibility for incorporating gender perspectives and ensuring the equal participation of women and men in all areas of work.

 

PLANNING AND ORGANIZING: Develops clear goals that are consistent with agreed strategies; identifies priority activities and assignments; adjusts priorities as required; allocates appropriate amount of time and resources for completing work; foresees risks and allows for contingencies when planning; monitors and adjusts plans and actions as necessary; uses time efficiently.

 

TEAMWORK: Works collaboratively with colleagues to achieve organizational goals; solicits input by genuinely valuing others' ideas and expertise; is willing to learn from others; places team agenda before personal agenda; supports and acts in accordance with final group decision, even when such decisions may not entirely reflect own position; shares credit for team accomplishments and accepts joint responsibility for team shortcomings.

Eligibility & Suitability Req
Education

A first-level university degree is required, preferably in human rights, law, political science, international relations, social sciences, economics, public policy, sustainable development, humanitarian affairs or related field.  


 

Work Experience 

A minimum of 2 years of experience in human rights or related areas is desirable. Relevant experience may include, but is not limited to political affairs, public affairs, international relations, law, social sciences, economics, development, humanitarian affairs, or other related areas. 

Working collaboratively is desirable.

Knowledge of human rights monitoring, documentation, analysis, research and reporting methodologies, including planning, coordinating and conducting field missions is desirable.

Skills in drafting human rights reports, policy and other documents is desirable.

Knowledge of regional and international human rights mechanisms is desirable.

Skills in conducting gender-, age- and trauma-sensitive human rights interviews is desirable.

Skills in designing, delivering, and evaluating capacity-building and training programmes is desirable.

Knowledge of international human rights law, norms, and standards is desirable.

Skill in applying and maintaining confidentiality, integrity, and availability principles is desirable.

Responsibilities
The Associate Human Rights Officer is responsible for conducting a wide range of tasks. Depending on the organizational setting, these may include monitoring and investigating compliance with international human rights and humanitarian law; analysing and managing data to inform early-warning, accountability and policy; drafting evidence-based reports and strategic advice; integrating human-rights perspectives into peace, humanitarian and development planning; advising UN leadership and national authorities on protection of civilians, rule of law and transitional justice; and fostering partnerships, capacity-building and advocacy initiatives that advance equality, accountability and the promise to leave no one behind.

Support the design and implementation of technical assistance programmes on human rights incorporating a gender perspective, providing analytical, coordination and substantive inputs.
Contribute to the organization of seminars, workshops and consultations aimed at strengthening institutional capacity and promoting gender‑responsive legislation and policies, including drafting background materials and tools.
Support fundraising and resource‑mobilization activities through preparatory research, drafting and coordination inputs to advance programme objectives.

Org. Setting and Reporting
This position is located in the Office of the High Commissioner for Human Rights (OHCHR) East and Southern Africa Regional Hub, in Addis Ababa, ETHIOPIA. The Associate Human Rights Officer reports to the Regional Director or his/her delegated representative.

The Associate Human Rights Officer is responsible for conducting a wide range of tasks. Depending on the organizational setting, these may include monitoring and investigating compliance with international human rights and humanitarian law; analysing and managing data to inform early-warning, accountability and policy; drafting evidence-based reports and strategic advice; integrating human-rights perspectives into peace, humanitarian and development planning; advising UN leadership and national authorities on protection of civilians, rule of law and transitional justice; and fostering partnerships, capacity-building and advocacy initiatives that advance equality, accountability and the promise to leave no one behind.

Responsibilities
Within delegated authority, the Associate Human Rights Officer will be responsible for the following duties: 

Monitor the implementation of international human rights law and international humanitarian law, and support follow-up on recommendations from human rights mechanisms, including treaty bodies, the Universal Periodic Review, special procedures and investigative mechanisms, to advance compliance and accountability.
Collect, document and analyse evidence-based information on civil, cultural, economic, social and political rights, including the right to development, within assigned thematic or geographic areas to support in-depth analysis, early warning, accountability, and informed decision-making, ensuring that human rights data is discoverable, interoperable and appropriately shared, including from gender and leave-no-one-behind perspectives.
Collect, verify and analyse multi-source information on thematic and country-specific human rights situations, ensuring data quality, methodological compliance and gender-responsive analysis.
Prepare analytical products, including lessons learned and trend analyses, to inform targeted human rights strategies, crisis prevention and response, and policy and programme development.
Contribute to the design and delivery of human rights capacity-building activities for State institutions, security forces, NHRIs, civil society and other stakeholders, including training on human rights-based approaches that integrate gender and leave-no-one-behind perspectives.
Provide professional support to authorities and non-State actors in integrating human rights standards into development and economic policies and programmes, including national development plans and Voluntary National and Local Reviews.
Provide technical support on administration of justice and security-sector issues, including trial and detention monitoring, transitional justice initiatives and vetting processes, through analysis and technical inputs.
Contribute to guidance and professional support to civil society actors on integrating human rights into protection, rule-of-law, development, transitional justice and accountability initiatives.
Provide research, coordination and follow-up support to treaty bodies, special procedures, the Universal Periodic Review and commissions of inquiry.
Provide professional research, analysis and policy advisory support on human rights issues related to economic policy and sustainable development, including poverty, inequality and development financing, contributing inputs to UN analytical and cooperation frameworks.
Support effective communication and public information efforts by contributing drafting, technical, and coordination inputs to amplify human rights messaging.
Contribute to the collection and analysis of data as well as identification of trends or patterns and provide draft insights through graphs, charts, tables and reports using data visualization methods for data-driven planning, decision-making, presentation and reporting.
Support the delivery of training programmes and workshops across multiple platforms for different audiences by providing technical content, facilitation support and logistical coordination.
Prepare and update training materials through research and drafting inputs in collaboration and consultation with relevant UN Agencies and other partners.
Competencies

Org. Setting and Reporting
This position is located in the Office of the High Commissioner for Human Rights (OHCHR), West Africa Regional Office, based in Dakar, Senegal. The Associate Human Rights Officer reports directly to the Deputy Regional Representative of the West Africa Regional Office (WARO).

The Associate Human Rights Officer is responsible for conducting a wide range of tasks. Depending on the organizational setting, these may include monitoring and investigating compliance with international human rights and humanitarian law; analysing and managing data to inform early-warning, accountability and policy; drafting evidence-based reports and strategic advice; integrating human-rights perspectives into peace, humanitarian and development planning; advising UN leadership and national authorities on protection of civilians, rule of law and transitional justice; and fostering partnerships, capacity-building and advocacy initiatives that advance equality, accountability and the promise to leave no one behind.

Responsibilities
Within delegated authority, the Associate Human Rights Officer will be responsible for the following duties: 

Monitor the implementation of international human rights law and international humanitarian law, and support follow-up on recommendations from human rights mechanisms, including treaty bodies, the Universal Periodic Review, special procedures and investigative mechanisms, to advance compliance and accountability.
Collect, document and analyse evidence-based information on civil, cultural, economic, social and political rights, including the right to development, within assigned thematic or geographic areas to support in-depth analysis, early warning, accountability, and informed decision-making, ensuring that human rights data is discoverable, interoperable and appropriately shared, including from gender and leave-no-one-behind perspectives.
Conduct human rights monitoring and field verification missions, including assessments and early-warning exercises, in line with UN standards and methodologies, and draft substantive analytical reports containing findings, trends and actionable recommendations.
Draft, review and contribute to human rights reports, briefings, legal and policy analyses and other communications, and prepare analytical papers that inform strategies, policies, programmes and decision-making, with integration of gender and leave-no-one-behind considerations.
Contribute to the design and delivery of human rights capacity-building activities for State institutions, security forces, NHRIs, civil society and other stakeholders, including training on human rights-based approaches that integrate gender and leave-no-one-behind perspectives.
Contribute to advocacy and participatory processes related to intergovernmental negotiations, public forums and peace processes, supporting the integration of human rights and anti-discrimination standards.
Provide research, coordination and follow-up support to treaty bodies, special procedures, the Universal Periodic Review and commissions of inquiry.
Support human rights advocacy and outreach initiatives through contributing to planning, content development, and implementation of visibility and awareness-raising activities.
Contribute to the collection and analysis of data as well as identification of trends or patterns and provide draft insights through graphs, charts, tables and reports using data visualization methods for data-driven planning, decision-making, presentation and reporting.
Maintain confidentiality and integrity and handle sensitive information with discretion, ensuring compliance with the UN data privacy and security requirements, and standards.
Support the delivery of training programmes and workshops across multiple platforms for different audiences by providing technical content, facilitation support, and logistical coordination.
Competencies
Professionalism: Knowledge of international human rights law, norms, and standards. Skills to integrate a human rights-based approach, leave no one behind, gender equality and women's empowerment into all areas of work. Knowledge of the application of the promotion and protection of economic, social and cultural rights, including within the context of addressing inequalities. Knowledge of human rights monitoring, documentation, analysis, research and reporting methodologies, including planning, coordinating and conducting field missions. Skills in drafting human rights reports, policy and other documents. Shows pride in work and in achievements; demonstrates professional competence and mastery of subject matter; is conscientious and efficient in meeting commitments, observing deadlines and achieving results; is motivated by professional rather than personal concerns; shows persistence when faced with difficult problems or challenges; remains calm in stressful situations.
